<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> For the pseudouridine-synthase protein represented by this sequence, which candidate residue is the catalytic nucleophile? Use one-based indexing to distinguish the nearby Asp candidates, and weigh site-specific catalytic evidence rather than relying on acidic-residue chemistry alone.<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> Asp77<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Asp102<br><br><strong>C.</strong> Asp86<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Asp104<br><br><strong>Skills:</strong> active_site_0000:1, active_site_0000:2<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>**Step 1: Identify the protein family**<br>This sequence corresponds to E. coli TruB (UniProt P60340), a Type I pseudouridine synthase (Ψ55 of 23S rRNA). TruB-type enzymes use an aspartate nucleophile to attack C1&#x27; of uridine, forming a covalent glycosyl-enzyme intermediate.<br><br>**Step 2: Locate the P-loop and catalytic motif**<br>TruB-type enzymes share a highly conserved P-loop (phosphate-binding loop) motif that harbors the catalytic aspartate. In this sequence, the conserved P-loop motif is at positions 72–82: &quot;VISAAQ**D**DKGR&quot;. The &quot;AAQDD&quot; segment is the hallmark P-loop catalytic motif of TruB-family enzymes.<br><br>**Step 3: Evaluate each candidate**<br><br>&#124; Candidate &#124; Context &#124; Role &#124;<br>&#124;-----------&#124;---------&#124;------&#124;<br>&#124; Asp77 &#124; &quot;AAQ**D**DK&quot; – first Asp of the conserved P-loop QDD motif &#124; Positioned directly in the nucleophilic attack site on C1′ of uridine &#124;<br>&#124; Asp86 &#124; &quot;VVT**D**FF&quot; – downstream of P-loop &#124; Structural role in the core fold; not in the nucleophilic geometry &#124;<br>&#124; Asp102 &#124; &quot;RL**D**YD&quot; &#124; Part of a distal loop; not aligned with uridine C1′ &#124;<br>&#124; Asp104 &#124; &quot;Y**D**TS&quot; &#124; Adjacent to Asp102 in the same distal loop; not catalytic &#124;<br><br>**Step 4: Structural and mutational evidence**<br>- Crystal structures of TruB (e.g., PDB 1K8W) place the catalytic Asp at the tip of the P-loop, directly contacting the uridine C1′.<br>- Site-directed mutagenesis of this Asp (the first D in the QDD motif) abolishes pseudouridylation activity.<br>- The residue is invariant across TruB orthologs, confirming its catalytic role.<br>- The other Asp residues (86, 102, 104) are in secondary structural elements that contribute to the overall fold but are not positioned for nucleophilic attack.<br><br>**Conclusion:** Asp77, located in the conserved P-loop motif &quot;AAQDD&quot;, is the catalytic nucleophile that performs the SN2-type attack on C1′ of uridine.<br><br>2. answer: A</td>
